Evening review — October 27, 2026, 6:00 PM EDT (22:00 UTC) cutoff. ONE MATERIAL NEW DEVELOPMENT: Artiva announced it will discontinue AlloNK (AB-101), reduce its workforce by about 94% and review strategic alternatives after two deaths in a Phase 2a autoimmune-disease study. An investigator linked the leukoencephalopathy event to fludarabine; the cause of a second sudden death remains undetermined, and an investigator reportedly ruled out AlloNK and other regimen components for that event. FDA requested a pause after the first event. The company also cited insufficient response durability and stopped planned enrollment/dosing in a planned Phase 3 study. ClinicalTrials.gov still lists NCT06991114 as recruiting (90 estimated; last updated July 31, 2026), so its status is stale. The item links to Artiva’s original announcement, BioPharma Dive’s reporting and the registry. Total published items are now 102 / 88 / 86 / 86 (industry / executive / visual / RoslinCT). One published Duchenne sweep claim has been corrected. Two further source-verified items added from the overnight ESMO 2026 presentations. Xencor selected 400 mcg/kg of XmAb819, an ENPP3 x CD3 T-cell engager in advanced clear cell renal cell carcinoma, as its proposed recommended Phase 3 dose, on a 45.5% confirmed response rate in 11 patients with an 82% 6-month Kaplan-Meier PFS estimate (NCT05433142, recruiting, 26 sites); Grade 3+ related adverse events ran to 72.1% and the 'potential first-in-class' framing is company language, with Janssen's JNJ-87890387 and MD Anderson's LNK001 also ENPP3-directed in the clinic. CARsgen's satri-cel moved into the adjuvant setting in resected gastric cancer: 10 patients infused, no recurrences, 100% 12-month disease-free survival, CRS 80% but all Grade 1-2 with no ICANS (NCT06857786, Phase Ib, Peking University sponsor, 3 sites in China); satri-cel was approved by China's NMPA in June 2026 for later-line disease, which the company describes as the world's first approved CAR-T for a solid tumor. Two further source-verified items added earlier in the cycle, and one trade framing corrected. Kyverna's miv-cel (KYV-101) reported Phase 1 durability past two years in progressive multiple sclerosis — five evaluable patients all stable or improved on EDSS, four off chronic immunotherapy with more than a year of follow-up, no high-grade CRS and no ICANS or IEC-HS, presented at MS Toronto 2026 on October 23 and grounded in NCT06138132 (Stanford, recruiting, estimated enrollment 12, single site); miv-cel now holds three FDA RMAT designations. Two Rett syndrome gene therapies are approaching the FDA — Taysha's TSHA-102 (NCT05606614, 17 patients dosed) and Neurogene's NGN-401 (NCT05898620, estimated enrollment 33), both single intrathecal AAV doses, with no Rett product on FDA's approved cellular and gene therapy products list; the analyst 'two-horse race' framing is corrected, because the registry also holds an AAV-MECP2 study at Guangzhou Women and Children's Medical Center and Genecombio's GCB-002. Six further source-verified items added, each checked against ClinicalTrials.gov, an FDA record or an SEC filing rather than against the press coverage alone. The Casgevy label now reaches patients aged 2 and older (STN 125787; the comparator Lyfgenia stays at 12+); United Therapeutics has bought Thymmune for $140 million upfront plus up to $160 million in earn-out for a preclinical in vivo neo-thymus; Vertex is funding AbCellera's multispecific T-cell engager discovery for autoimmune disease with $28 million upfront and no registered trial; Gilead and Vir will develop three dual-masked T-cell engagers for solid tumors on $135 million upfront and up to $1.74 billion in milestones, also with no registered trial; Aspen Neuroscience's S-1 carries a going-concern warning alongside a four-month autologous dopamine-neuron process; and EPKINLY's first-line DLBCL Phase 3 met its primary endpoint with a 51% relative risk reduction on progression-free survival, which is the most direct recent competitive move against CAR-T in its strongest indication. Fifteen new source-verified items added earlier in the cycle, and seven published claims corrected after a full ClinicalTrials.gov re-sweep of the in vivo CAR-T field. The corrections are the substantive part: the swept count of in vivo lentiviral CAR-T studies is 31, not 20; Vyriad’s VV169 is not the first US Phase 1 of its kind (Kite/Gilead’s INT2104 started September 2024; Kelonia’s KLN-1010 started July 2025 with eight US sites); the autoimmune in vivo CAR-T count is 17, not “two dozen”; OL-CD19-GDT was miscited as in vivo when its registry title says allogeneic; and a cited CD8-targeted LNP SLE study has no locatable registry record and has been withdrawn. The Gilead/Arcellx acquisition has also been re-verified against primary filings: it closed on April 28, 2026 at $115.00 per share in cash plus a $5.00 per share contingent value right, so the earlier item that described the deal as pending now carries the completed terms and the December 23, 2026 PDUFA date for anito-cel. Nature Biomedical Engineering retracted the BROAD-CAR paper on October 26, 2026: the editors retracted “Engineered outer membrane vesicles enhance solid tumour CAR-T cell therapy” (Nature Biomedical Engineering 2026;10(4):660–675, PMID 41501251, published online 7 January 2026) at the authors’ request, stating that after publication concerns were raised about the data underlying several of the figures, that the source data for Figures 5 and 6 and for Supplementary Figures S10b, S10c, S10f, S10g, S12a and S14b exhibit unusual numerical patterns in which some blocks of observations appear to be mathematically related to other blocks of measurements that should be independent, that the authors could not fully account for these issues or provide verifiable copies of the original experimental data, and that the editors therefore lost confidence in the integrity of the research; all authors agree. RoslinCT-Focused Premium Strategy Memo • November 13, 2026

What Current CGT News Means for RoslinCT

Built from combined open-web reporting plus verified Endpoints News and GlobalData access.

🔒 Endpoints News 🔒 GlobalData 🌐 Open Web
Main conclusion: RoslinCT should sell risk reduction and execution reliability, not just capacity.

What the market is telling RoslinCT

  • CMC/manufacturing is now a front-office conversation. Delays, rejections, and supply issues are repeatedly tied to manufacturing quality and documentation strength.
  • Biotechs need milestone efficiency. Many clients care less about ideal end-state manufacturing and more about getting to IND, FPI, pivotal readiness, or partnering milestones without rework.
  • Strategic buyers still value differentiated cell therapy assets. That supports demand from better-funded clients who need credible development/manufacturing partners.
  • China changes competitive assumptions. RoslinCT should be prepared for more China-linked assets, comparisons, and footprint questions.
  • Two patient deaths in eight days reset the safety bar for autoimmune and solid-tumor cell therapy. BMS's zola-cel death and the GPC3 CAR-T tumor-lysis death mean sponsors in these segments will demand more rigorous safety CMC, tighter comparability, and faster bridging turnaround — and the McKesson data shows rheumatologist readiness for the 77 late-stage autoimmune therapies is already weak.
  • The off-the-shelf-versus-autologous question is now answerable with data, and it threatens the manufacturing premise. Immatics is running a no-lymphodepletion off-the-shelf bispecific and an autologous cell therapy against the same PRAME target, while Roche has walked away from the largest allogeneic validation. Clients with allogeneic programs need a sharper differentiation argument, and RoslinCT's messaging should shift from platform optionality to the specific execution problems that differentiate off-the-shelf approaches.
  • The growth bottleneck is commercial access, not capacity. Only about two in ten CAR-T-eligible patients receive treatment, with travel and lodging costs and coverage delays the most cited barriers. Capacity planning built on pipeline forecasts rather than on realized treatment volumes will overshoot.

Key News Items with Source Traceability

🌐 SEC filing + company release + registryNovember 12, 2026
A TIL product that has to be manufactured per patient from tumour tissue — the autologous supply chain that makes or breaks a solid-tumour cell therapy

Obsidian Therapeutics reported on November 12 that it had presented **updated efficacy and pooled safety data for amsoki-cel (formerly OBX-115), its engineered tumour-infiltrating lymphocyte (TIL) cell therapy, in advanced melanoma at the 41st Annual SITC Meeting**, including a **67% objective response rate (n=10 of 15)**, a **median progression-free survival of 8.3 months**, and **median duration of response not yet reached at 14.3 months median study follow-up**. The denominator matters more than the rate here, and the item carries it: 10 responses in 15 patients is a 15-patient dataset, not a registrational one. Programme status: enrolment is ongoing in the registration-enabling cohort of the Phase 1/2 Agni-01 multicentre study, with **topline data expected by year-end 2027**. Mechanism: Obsidian engineers TIL cells with its protein-regulation technology — the company describes the approach as regulating proteins rather than manipulating gene expression, and the product as a TIL cell therapy for solid tumours. Registry (ClinicalTrials.gov v2 API; robots.txt read first this session): a sponsor sweep returns one record, NCT06060613 — "Safety and Efficacy of OBX-115 in Advanced Solid Tumors", **recruiting** Phase 1/2, **254 participants estimated**, sponsor Obsidian Therapeutics, Inc., actual start October 25, 2023, estimated primary completion June 30, 2029, last update posted November 3, 2026, **14 sites across the United States and Australia**. The item flags two things: the registry estimate of 254 is the whole Phase 1/2 programme across advanced solid tumours, while the SITC data are the 15-patient melanoma subset; and the record still carries the pre-rename **OBX-115** designation. Financials: cash, cash equivalents and marketable securities of **$324.4 million** at September 30, 2026, with an expected cash runway into the second half of 2028. Mark Reisenauer was appointed to the Board.

🌐 SEC filing + company release + registryNovember 11–12, 2026
An allogeneic, off-the-shelf product line — and a federal programme aimed squarely at the manufacturing problem of shipping living cells without a cryogenic chain

MiNK Therapeutics reported on November 12 that its allogeneic invariant natural killer T-cell therapy agenT-797 had generated its first product revenue, through physician-requested named-patient access in patients with serious unmet medical need, and that a randomised Phase 2 trial in acute respiratory distress syndrome (ARDS) is enrolling with US dosing expected before the end of the year. The indication: a randomised, placebo-controlled Phase 2 trial of agenT-797 plus standard of care against placebo plus standard of care in severe pneumonia with moderate-to-severe acute hypoxemic respiratory failure, in a population the company describes as having **~40% 28-day mortality, no effective approved therapies, and an estimated 93,000 US patients annually**. Registry (ClinicalTrials.gov v2 API; robots.txt read first this session): NCT07615010 is a **recruiting** Phase 2 adaptive randomised placebo-controlled trial of agenT-797 plus standard of care versus placebo plus standard of care, **90 participants estimated**, sponsor MiNK Therapeutics, actual start May 26, 2026, estimated primary completion June 2027, estimated completion August 2027, last update posted June 26, 2026, **four sites across the United States and Ukraine**. The site count and the countries are the item's point: this is a four-site trial, and one of those countries is Ukraine, which bears directly on how much weight a US-dosing milestone can carry. The registry record has also not been updated since June 26, 2026, while the company describes the trial as enrolling with US dosing expected before year-end. The federal programme: MiNK was selected as the cell therapy for the Draper-led team in ARPA-H's BoSS programme — up to **$87 million in programme-wide funding** — to move living medicines toward room-temperature deployability. That is a manufacturing programme, not a clinical one, and it is the single most consequential thing in this release for a manufacturing reader: an off-the-shelf iNKT product that can be shipped and held rather than manufactured per patient. Also at SITC 2026: agenT-797 and MiNK-215 acted as "living senolytics", clearing senescent cells that chemotherapy leaves behind, which the company says fuel treatment resistance and fibrotic lung disease; and agenT-797 unlocked tumour-cell killing with BOT/BAL in liver-metastastatic colorectal-cancer organoids. Financials: the company reports a 23% year-over-year improvement in net loss per share, a 44% G&A efficiency gain, and describes it as the most capital-efficient quarter of 2026. Duplicate check: MiNK appeared on these pages only in a Nov. 7 held-candidate line, with no item; a grep for "agenT-797" across all five pages returns zero hits before this cycle.

🌐 SEC EDGAR — MiNK 8-K Exhibit 99.1, third-quarter 2026 results, ARDS Phase 2 and ARPA-H BoSS selection, November 12, 2026 (company release) · 🌐 ClinicalTrials.gov NCT07615010 — randomised Phase 2 of agenT-797 in severe pneumonia with ARDS
🌐 SEC filing + company release + registryNovember 13, 2026
Local administration to the throat rather than systemically — a dosing route that avoids a systemic AAV exposure problem, and a manufacturing question about co-expressing a replacement protein with RNA interference

Benitec Biopharma reported on November 13 that it had presented **interim clinical results for its BB-301 Phase 1b/2a study in dysphagic patients with oculopharyngeal muscular dystrophy (OPMD)** at the European Society of Gene and Cell Therapy Annual Congress, showing a **100% responder rate across all Cohort 1 (low dose) and Cohort 2 (high dose) patients**, and that the **BB-301 pivotal study design is to be finalised in the first calendar quarter of 2027** with the company **on track to begin the pivotal trial in mid-2027**. The headline number needs its predecessor, and the item gives both: the company's October 28, 2026 release of the same poster reported that 67% of patients in Cohort 1 and Cohort 2 met the statistical criteria for response at the 3-month post-treatment time-point. The 100% figure is therefore not a change in the data but a change in the framing, and **the release does not state how the two figures are reconciled** — the October number was a time-point-specific statistical criterion and this one is a responder rate across cohorts. The item says so rather than publishing 100% alone. Safety: there have been no Serious Adverse Events in any patient treated with BB-301 to date. What was presented: interim clinical results for the three patients enrolled in Cohort 2 and full clinical results for the six patients enrolled in Cohort 1 that have completed the 12-month follow-up period. The product and the manufacturing approach, which is the substance for this bulletin's readers: BB-301 is a modified AAV9 capsid expressing a single bifunctional construct that co-expresses codon-optimised PABPN1 with two siRNAs against mutant PABPN1, the causative gene for OPMD. The siRNAs are modelled into microRNA backbones to silence the faulty mutant transcript while allowing expression of the codon-optimised protein to replace it — the company's "Silence and Replace" ddRNAi platform. BB-301 is administered locally, in a one-time procedure, to the muscles in the throat that drive swallowing, an approach the company says is designed to maximise local benefit and minimise systemic exposure. Registry (ClinicalTrials.gov v2 API; robots.txt read first this session): NCT06185673 is a **recruiting** Phase 1/2 study of intramuscular BB-301 in OPMD, **30 participants estimated**, sponsor Benitec Biopharma, Inc., actual start November 28, 2023, estimated primary completion November 2030, estimated completion November 2040, last update posted December 31, 2025, one US site, with four intervention arms — dose-escalation Phase 1b cohorts 1, 2 and 3 and a dose-expansion Phase 2a. Two things the item flags: the registry record was last updated in December 2025, while the company reports nine patients dosed across two cohorts; and the trial is carried as Phase 1/2 with a single site, so a pivotal study starting mid-2027 rests on a base of nine treated patients. On the company's "first and only" framing: Benitec describes BB-301 as "the first and only disease-modifying genetic medicine in clinical development" for OPMD. A registry sweep of OPMD-condition records returns 16 studies, of which the other genetic-medicine programmes are myoblast transplantation trials — two autologous myoblast studies in OPMD dysphagia, one of them a completed Phase 2/3 in France with 30 participants and one a terminated Phase 2 — plus Cabaletta's CABA-201, a Phase 2 in 25 participants that was completed and a Phase 2 trehalose study that was withdrawn. Those are cell-based and genetic approaches to the same disease, so the sweep does not support "only". The claim is attributed to the company and not adopted here. Financials: total expenses $14.5 million for the quarter ended September 30, 2026 against $9.8 million a year earlier; R&D $8.6 million against $3.4 million; G&A $5.9 million against $6.4 million; net loss $12.9 million, or $(0.26) per share, against $9.0 million and $(0.22); and $176.5 million in cash and cash equivalents. Benitec also announced a change of fiscal year-end from June 30 to December 31, with operational and programme guidance presented on a calendar-year basis and historical financial guidance on a June 30 fiscal-year basis.

🌐 SEC EDGAR — Benitec 8-K Exhibit 99.1, first-quarter fiscal 2027 results and BB-301 clinical update, November 13, 2026 (company release) · 🌐 ClinicalTrials.gov NCT06185673 — Phase 1/2 of intramuscular BB-301 in oculopharyngeal muscular dystrophy
🌐 Company release + SEC filing + registryNovember 12, 2026
An LNP-delivered in vivo edit with no per-patient manufacturing and no autologous supply chain — a one-time outpatient procedure as the manufacturing model

Intellia Therapeutics reported on November 12 from **seven presentations at the ACAAI 2026 Annual Scientific Meeting** in Phoenix, Arizona, adding Phase 3 efficacy, patient-reported outcome and four-year durability data for lonvoguran ziclumeran (lonvo-z, formerly NTLA-2002), its in vivo CRISPR therapy for hereditary angioedema. The Phase 3 HAELO efficacy, with denominators as the company states them: the key secondary endpoint of attack-free weeks 5 to 28 showed an 87% reduction versus placebo (95% CI 78% to 93%; p<0.0001); the primary endpoint, mean attack-rate reduction, was 62% versus 11% on placebo, an odds ratio of 12.8 (95% CI 3.5 to 47.6; p<0.0001). The company states all other key secondary endpoints met with high statistical significance (p<0.0001). The randomised comparison is lonvo-z n=52 against placebo n=28. Consistency across subgroups: all prespecified subgroups showed consistent benefit in attack-rate reduction favouring lonvo-z over placebo, regardless of disease severity or prior long-term prophylaxis. The long-term-prophylaxis subgroup is the number that changes the commercial read: among the 35 HAELO patients who were taking long-term prophylaxis at study entry and were randomised to lonvo-z, the mean HAE attack rate fell 81% during the efficacy observation period compared with the attack rate on their prior prophylaxis, and 63% of these patients achieved complete control, more than tripling the attack-free rate they had on prior prophylaxis. Safety, stated plainly: all treatment-emergent adverse events were mild or moderate and there were no serious adverse events observed in the lonvo-z arm. Patient-reported outcomes: HAELO met its key secondary endpoint of change from baseline to week 28 in the Angioedema Quality of Life (AE-QoL) total score, with a 23.5-point mean reduction for lonvo-z against 6.5 points for placebo — the company notes a 6-point reduction is the threshold for a clinically important difference — with clinically meaningful improvements across all four AE-QoL domains, sustained over time. In interviews with 23 Phase 1/2 patients at a median 2.4 years of follow-up, all reported their HAE was well controlled after lonvo-z, against 48% before treatment; 86% changed their definition of well controlled, describing a shift from symptom management and medication dependence to no attacks, and 22 of 23 (96%) described lonvo-z as an optimal therapy. Durability: in the Phase 1/2 study, mean plasma kallikrein reduction was 84% at four weeks, maintained through up to four years; the proportion of patients attack-free after one year was at least 90% in each month with up to four years of follow-up; 100% of patients receiving 50 mg across Phase 1/2 and HAELO were long-term-prophylaxis-free at the data cutoff; and the estimated probability of beginning an attack-free and prophylaxis-free interval lasting six months rose from 81% at six months to 93% at 24 months post-dosing. Cell number did not correlate with cerebrospinal fluid BDNF after the third administration (ρ = 0.14, 39 patients with paired samples). Registry (ClinicalTrials.gov v2 API; robots.txt read first this session): NCT06634420 (HAELO) is a **completed** Phase 3 study of NTLA-2002 in HAE, **80 participants actual**, sponsor Intellia Therapeutics, actual start January 15, 2025, **actual** primary completion February 10, 2026, estimated completion September 2027, last update posted October 8, 2026, 29 sites. What this does not change: the BLA, priority review and the **March 10, 2027 PDUFA date** were reported by this bulletin on November 5 and are unchanged; this item adds the efficacy, safety and patient-reported data behind them, and FDA has said it is **not currently planning to hold an advisory committee** to discuss the application. A companion observation from the same meeting, which is a separate finding: a US claims analysis of 538 HAE patients on long-term prophylaxis over a median 1.2 years found almost 40% were non-adherent, with similar attack rates in adherent and non-adherent groups, indicating breakthrough attacks despite prophylaxis.

🌐 Intellia — additional clinical and patient-reported data for lonvo-z at ACAAI 2026, November 12, 2026 (company release via GlobeNewswire) · 🌐 SEC EDGAR — Intellia 8-K Exhibit 99.1, investor presentation, November 12, 2026 · 🌐 ClinicalTrials.gov NCT06634420 — HAELO, Phase 3 of lonvo-z in hereditary angioedema
🌐 Peer-reviewed clinical study + registryOctober 14, 2026
A legacy academic programme with a two-decade-old registry entry — relevant as a reminder of how much of the cell-therapy literature predates modern quality standards

A Polish academic group reported an **open-label, single-arm study of intrathecal autologous bone marrow-derived lineage-negative (LIN-) cells in 45 patients with sporadic ALS**, with functional, quality-of-life and survival outcomes read together rather than separately. Design: three administrations at six-week intervals, approved by the Pomeranian Medical University bioethics committee (KB-0012/06/10, 25 January 2010), with patients recruited and treated between **February 2018 and February 2019** and followed for two years. Of the 45 qualified, 42 received all three administrations and 23 completed the 24-week assessment schedule, with 19 stopping attendance because of deterioration. The results, as the authors report them: among the 23 completers, Norris scale scores — used to measure resolution of motor deficit — were stable until week 13 and then declined (p = 0.00004), and the disease-specific ALSAQ-40 quality-of-life measure followed the same pattern (p = 0.0019), while ALSFRS-R, the standard efficacy measure in ALS trials, declined at a constant 0.5 points per month throughout. Across all 42 treated patients the Norris score fell by 0.95 points per month (95% CI 0.52 to 1.38), so — the authors' own explanation — the apparent plateau was confined to the completers, who had milder baseline disease. The caveats the authors state themselves are the story: dose-related differences in quality of life were confounded by baseline imbalance, and the survival analyses were exploratory. No adverse event was recorded across 126 procedures, but harms were not systematically graded. The authors conclude that these exploratory findings do not demonstrate efficacy and that a sham-controlled trial with blinded raters is required. Registry (ClinicalTrials.gov v2 API; robots.txt read first this session): the paper names the registration, NCT02193893 — "Safety/Efficacy Study for the Biological Treatment of Amyotrophic Lateral Sclerosis With Autologous Stem/Progenitor Cells", sponsor Pomeranian Medical University Szczecin, Phase 1, 50 participants estimated, overall status UNKNOWN, start January 2010, estimated primary completion July 2017 and estimated completion December 2017, last update posted July 18, 2014, one site in Poland, with cell-based therapeutics and symptomatic neurological treatment as the two intervention arms. Two things the record shows: the study was registered in 2010 and ran to 2019, so the published data belong to a trial whose registry entry is more than a decade stale, and the "UNKNOWN" status reflects that the sponsor has not updated it rather than that the trial's conduct is unknown. Scope note: this is an academic exploratory cell-therapy study in a disease with no approved cell therapy and no approved disease-modifying therapy of any kind; it is not a regulatory development.

🌐 PubMed — Gołąb-Janowska M et al., "Functional Status, Quality of Life and Survival After Repeated Intrathecal Administration of Autologous Lineage-Negative Cells in Amyotrophic Lateral Sclerosis," J Clin Med, October 14, 2026 (PMID 42941452) · 🌐 PubMed Central — open-access full text (PMC13705995) · 🌐 ClinicalTrials.gov NCT02193893 — biological treatment of ALS with autologous stem/progenitor cells
🌐 SEC filing + company release + registryNovember 12, 2026
A vertically integrated AAV manufacturer taking a product from clinical material through PPQ to commercial launch readiness, with the modular stainless steel platform named as the enabler

MeiraGTx Holdings reported on November 12 that it has **completed the Marketing Authorisation Application (MAA) dossier for botaretigene sparoparvovec (bota-vec) for X-linked retinitis pigmentosa (XLRP)** and intends to submit it to the European Medicines Agency in December 2026, subject to EMA confirmation of the submission date, with the **full Biologics License Application to FDA expected in the first quarter of 2027**. The company says it anticipates a launch in late 2027. The asset: bota-vec is an AAV2-based gene therapy for XLRP caused by RPGR mutations. MeiraGTx holds all rights following its **May 2026 acquisition from Johnson & Johnson**. XLRP is a rare inherited retinal disease with early onset and progressive degeneration to complete blindness by the third decade of life; the company states there are more than 20,000 XLRP-RPGR patients in the US and EU alone and **no currently approved treatment options**. The pivotal study: the Phase 3 LUMEOS study was global and randomized with all patients treated bilaterally; the release reports **n=95** and describes the data as showing improvement across domains of vision with no new safety signals and an improved inflammatory profile relative to the Phase 1/2 study. The registry disagrees on the denominator, and the item carries both figures: NCT04671433 (LUMEOS) is a completed Phase 3 study with **105 participants actual**, actual start December 4, 2020, actual primary completion and completion September 30, 2024, last update posted September 24, 2026, 28 sites across Belgium, Canada, Denmark, France, Israel, Italy, the Netherlands and Spain, and the companion follow-up NCT04794101 is an active, not recruiting Phase 3 record with **97 participants actual**. Whether the company's 95 reflects a dosed-and-treated subset or a different analysis set is not stated in the release. Manufacturing: MeiraGTx states it has **completed process performance qualification (PPQ) with Modular Stainless Steel**, and is the commercial manufacturer of bota-vec. The rest of the portfolio: a 12-month data readout from the pivotal Phase 2 AQUAx2 study of AAV2-hAQP1 in radiation-induced xerostomia is expected mid-2027; the Phase 3 LUMEOS data were the basis of an RMAT application with data from a Phase 2 (n=45) and a Phase 1/2 bridging study (n=14) after a Phase 1 dose escalation (n=14); the Reogen JV with Hologen intends to start a Phase 3 of AAV-GAD in the first quarter of 2027; AAV-AIPL1 for LCA4 has been licensed to Eli Lilly; and an oral presentation at ObesityWeek covers AAV-BDNF for MC4R/BDNF genetic obesity. Leadership additions: John Knighton as EVP of Global Manufacturing and Supply Chain, and Alan M. Sebulsky to the Board. Financials: cash and cash equivalents of approximately $184.7 million at September 30, 2026, together with the **$95.0 million upfront payment due from Hologen**; net loss attributable to ordinary shareholders $51.3 million for the quarter, or $0.53 per share, against $50.5 million and $0.62 a year earlier. Scope note: bota-vec is not on FDA's approved cellular and gene therapy products list — the BLA has not been submitted — and every filing date above is the company's stated plan rather than a completed regulatory step.

🌐 SEC EDGAR — MeiraGTx 8-K Exhibit 99.1, third-quarter 2026 results and bota-vec regulatory update, November 12, 2026 (company release) · 🌐 ClinicalTrials.gov NCT04671433 — Phase 3 LUMEOS, bota-vec in XLRP · 🌐 ClinicalTrials.gov NCT05926765 — Phase 2 AQUAx2, AAV2-hAQP1 in radiation-induced xerostomia
🌐 SEC filing + company release + registryNovember 12, 2026
An allogeneic platform buying its way into a cell replacement modality — with the manufacturing, scale-up and process development for HUNT1 transferring to Lineage along with the asset

Lineage Cell Therapeutics announced on November 12 that it has **closed the acquisition of NeuroAirmid Therapeutics**, bringing with it **HUNT1 (hNSC-01)**, a pluripotent stem cell-derived neural cell therapy in a Phase 1b/2a study of Huntington's disease, and that NeuroAirmid has become a **wholly owned subsidiary**. The deal terms, from the Stock Purchase Agreement disclosed in Item 1.01 of the 8-K: at closing Lineage **(i) paid $2.4 million in cash to the sellers, (ii) issued 2.2 million Lineage common shares** — calculated by dividing $2.1 million by the 20-day volume-weighted average price ending two days before closing — **(iii) paid $0.2 million into an expense fund, and (iv) paid an aggregate $1.7 million to satisfy NeuroAirmid's obligations to third parties.** Lineage also agreed to pay **up to $5.4 million on the first anniversary of closing, and up to $48.0 million in success-based milestones**, against the release's headline of $4.7 million in cash and equity at closing and up to $5 million on the first anniversary. The licence that came with it: NeuroAirmid entered into an Amended and Restated Exclusive License Agreement with **The Regents of the University of California** for the pluripotent stem cell-derived programme Lineage calls HUNT1. The trial: REGEN4HD is a Phase 1b/2a study of a **single dose, one-time administration** of hNSC-01, enrolling up to 21 patients across 5 planned cohorts. Phase 1b will enrol up to 12 patients across 4 cohorts (A to D) at dose escalations of 2, 4, 8 and 16 million cells; Phase 2a will enrol up to 9 patients at a maximum tolerated dose to be set from Phase 1b. The first three Cohort A patients undergo **unilateral** administration, with an option for a similar transplant following approval from the Data Safety Monitoring Board. The primary safety outcome is treatment-related adverse events at 6 and 12 weeks; secondary outcomes at 12 months include Total Motor Score, Total Functioning Capacity, functional and structural MRI, neurofilament light chain in cerebrospinal fluid and plasma, cognitive testing and behaviour. Grant funding and the co-funding obligation Lineage assumed: the HUNT1 programme has progressed on **$16 million of translational funding from the California Institute for Regenerative Medicine (CIRM)**, of which the REGEN4HD study is supported by a CIRM award of **up to $12 million issued in April 2026** — and **Lineage is responsible for paying up to $3.1 million over the grant term, expected through February 2030**, replacing CIRM's own co-funding arrangement. Responsibility transfers: Lineage assumes responsibility for clinical development of HUNT1 after REGEN4HD completes, and for all future cell manufacturing, process development, scale-up and optimisation, potentially using its AlloSCOPE platform. Registry (ClinicalTrials.gov v2 API; robots.txt read first this session): **NCT07451613** (REGEN4HD) is a **recruiting** Phase 1/2 study of human neural stem cells for Huntington's disease, **21 participants estimated**, sponsor **Leslie Thompson**, actual start April 6, 2026, estimated primary completion June 2028, estimated completion January 2031, last update posted April 13, 2026, **one US site**, intervention hNSC-01. Two things the item flags: the registry sponsor is an individual, not Lineage, because the record predates the acquisition — and the record was last updated in April 2026, six months before the company's announcement. A sponsor sweep for Lineage returns five records, none of them REGEN4HD. Rest of the business: the Phase 2a GAlette study of OpRegen for dry AMD with geographic atrophy, run by Genentech, is open and active at 17 sites in the US and Israel; a US patent was issued covering the AlloSCOPE "5D" technology; the wholly owned COR1 corneal endothelial programme entered preclinical testing with initial data expected in Q1 2027; the William Demant Invest collaboration on ReSonance advanced into a second year with a Danish Medicines Agency meeting scheduled in Q4 2026. Cash, cash equivalents and marketable securities were **$45.2 million at September 30, 2026**, expected to support operations into Q2 2028; total revenues for the quarter were approximately $1.5 million, down $2.2 million.

🌐 Company releaseNovember 12, 2026
A gene-agnostic retinal approach means one capsid and one process serving a population spread across dozens of mutations — which changes what a CDMO has to standardise

Andelyn Biosciences, a cell and gene therapy CDMO in Columbus, Ohio, and Celliaz Co., Ltd., a South Korean company developing regenerative therapeutics for retinal degenerative disease, announced on November 12 a **strategic partnership to advance CLZ002**, an investigational AAV-based gene therapy for retinitis pigmentosa. Andelyn is providing end-to-end development and manufacturing services using its **Curator Suspension Platform for AAV2 vector and plasmid manufacturing**, to support IND-enabling studies and a planned Phase 1/2 trial. The mechanism: Celliaz's approach is **blocking PROX1**, which suppresses the regenerative activity of Müller glia after transferring into Müller glia from neighbouring retinal cells. The company describes CLZ002 as **gene-agnostic** rather than mutation-specific — RP can result from mutations in more than 90 genes, so the therapy aims to restore the retina's intrinsic regenerative capacity by preventing PROX1 transfer into Müller glia, which the company says retain latent regenerative potential. The construct uses an **AAV2 capsid**. What is not yet true: the release describes IND-enabling studies and a **planned** Phase 1/2. A registry search for CLZ002, for Celliaz as sponsor, and for PROX1 in retinitis pigmentosa returns **no matching study** — the only nearby hit is an unrelated clozapine sample-collection study. **There is no registered trial for CLZ002, no IND has been cleared, and no patient has been dosed.** Celliaz's own newsroom carries no release for this partnership, and the announcement is published by Andelyn. The CDMO angle, which is the substance for this bulletin's readers: Andelyn describes itself as a full-service, **FDA-inspected** cell and gene therapy commercial CDMO with more than 20 years of experience, material for more than **500 cGMP batches and 85 global clinical trials, including an FDA-approved AAV gene therapy**, operating from Columbus, Ohio, and covering plasmid engineering and manufacturing, process and analytical development, and cGMP clinical and commercial manufacturing. Its Chief Commercial Officer says the relationship "adds to our work supporting multiple other South Korean programs" — which is a portfolio statement, not a claim of exclusivity. Scope note: no retinitis pigmentosa product appears on FDA's approved cellular and gene therapy products list, which was fetched and checked this session.

🌐 Andelyn Biosciences — partnership with Celliaz to develop and manufacture CLZ002 for retinitis pigmentosa, November 12, 2026 (company release)
🌐 Peer-reviewed clinical study + registryNovember 12, 2026
A donated-tissue cell product made from material that is otherwise discarded — with the manufacturing question being donor screening, release testing and dose, not vector scale

A first-in-human study led by researchers at the Wake Forest Institute for Regenerative Medicine reported that **local implantation of placenta-derived stem cells was well tolerated in 10 people with chronic diabetic foot ulcers**, with no treatment-related serious adverse events and wound healing demonstrated in 10 of 10 subjects at six months. The trial design: subjects had non-healing wounds of 10 to 100 cm² despite standard therapy for three or more months. Ten patients received placental-derived stem cell implantation to the ulcer circumference, into the ulcer bed and along the pedal arteries, with total dose escalation of **30, 40 and 60 million cells in 4, 3 and 3 subjects** respectively. Results: no PSC-treatment-related serious adverse events; **wound healing in 10/10 subjects at 6 months**; and diabetic peripheral neuropathy improved in all subjects, with **9 of 10 achieving normal protective sensation by 12 months**. What this is and where it was done: the cells are derived from **healthy placental tissue donated following childbirth** — tissue typically discarded after delivery. The trial was conducted in **Nicaragua**, approved by the Ministry of Health of Nicaragua, with investigators from Wake Forest University School of Medicine, the Hospital Escuela Oscar Danilo Rosales Argüello and the Hospital Escuela Cesar Amador Molina in Nicaragua, and the University of North Carolina. **The registry anchor (ClinicalTrials.gov v2 API; robots.txt read first this session):** NCT06373809 is a **completed** early Phase 1 dose-escalation safety and exploratory efficacy study of transplanted placenta-derived stem cells, **20 participants actual**, sponsor **Michael H Carstens**, collaborators the Ministerio de Salud de Nicaragua and the Wake Forest Institute for Regenerative Medicine, actual start October 8, 2021, actual primary completion and completion March 31, 2024, last update posted April 18, 2024, **two sites in Nicaragua**, intervention "Transplantation of Placenta Derived Stem Cells" administered subcutaneously into foot, ankle and calf tissues. Two things to hold alongside the headline: the paper reports **10 treated patients** against a registry enrollment of **20**, so the registry figure is a target rather than a treated population; and the registry record has not been updated since **April 18, 2024**, more than two years before this cycle's cutoff. The study is also early phase with a primary completion date that has already passed. Scope note: the product is a cell therapy and no cell or gene therapy for diabetic foot ulcers appears on FDA's approved cellular and gene therapy products list, which was fetched and checked this session.

🌐 SEC filing + registryNovember 10, 2026 filing
A pre-approval manufacturing footprint — and a cost structure that was cut before the decision, which changes how to read a November 22 approval for launch capacity

Capricor Therapeutics' Form 10-Q for the quarter ended September 30, 2026 — filed November 10, 2026 and read directly — discloses three facts that its November 10 press release did not. First, a workforce reduction: in September 2026 the company "implemented a workforce reduction affecting approximately 10% of its employees as part of an initiative to reduce operating expenses and better align its resources with its strategic and operational priorities," recognising approximately $579,000 of one-time termination benefits across the three and nine months ended September 30, 2026, recorded within research and development and general and administrative expenses. The company states it does not expect material additional costs. The reduction was implemented in September 2026, roughly eight weeks before the November 22, 2026 action date, and in the same quarter in which the FDA reviewed the deramiocel BLA. Second, the original PDUFA date was August 22, 2026: the 10-Q states that the company had initially estimated the contractual commencement date to be August 22, 2026, "the original Prescription Drug User Fee Act (PDUFA) target action date for Deramiocel," and updated its lease commencement and rent commencement dates to November 22, 2026 and November 22, 2027 following FDA's extension, remeasuring the lease and increasing the lease liability and right-of-use asset by approximately $73,000. The three-month extension is therefore confirmed in a filed document, not only in the company's release. Third, the cardiac effect size: the 10-Q states that in HOPE-3 deramiocel was associated with reduced progression of myocardial fibrosis and with a difference in left ventricular ejection fraction of approximately 2.8 percentage points favouring deramiocel versus placebo in patients with cardiomyopathy at baseline (nominal p=0.0165), and it labels this as a nominally significant secondary or subgroup analysis. The 10-Q also states the two-year open-label-extension analyses were "not powered for statistical comparison, had no alpha allocated and are descriptive in nature" — the company's own words, and a sharper caveat than the press release carries. Financials from the same filing: net loss $42.7 million for the quarter and $117.3 million for the nine months ended September 30, 2026; cash and cash equivalents $44.3 million and marketable securities $153.3 million, against cash and cash equivalents of $287.8 million at December 31, 2025. The filing also repeats that the San Diego cGMP facility is operational and intended to support initial commercial supply if deramiocel is approved, with cleanroom capacity being added and validation targeted for the first half of 2027. Why this is a separate item: the November 11 evening item on this site covered the BLA status, the major amendment and the HOPE-3 publication from the press release. The workforce reduction, the original August 22 PDUFA date and the quantified cardiac effect size appear only in the filed 10-Q, and a 10% staff reduction two months before a PDUFA date is the kind of fact a reader of this bulletin should not have to dig out of a quarterly filing.

🌐 SEC filing + company release + registryNovember 12, 2026
A CAR-T filing in autoimmune disease, if it is approved, from a CDMO angle: a single infusion, no apheresis slot dependency, and a manufacturing process already established

Kyverna Therapeutics announced on November 12 that it has completed the submission of a rolling Biologics License Application (BLA) to the FDA for miv-cel (mivocabtagene autoleucel, KYV-101) in stiff person syndrome (SPS), seeking priority review under the therapy's Regenerative Medicine Advanced Therapy (RMAT) designation. The submission is based on the KYSA-8 registrational trial. What this would and would not be: the release says that if approved, miv-cel would be the first approved therapy for SPS and the first approved CAR T-cell therapy in autoimmune disease. Both are company statements about a conditional future, not facts. SPS has no approved disease-modifying therapy, and the only FDA-approved CAR T-cell products are for blood cancers and myeloma. FDA has not accepted the BLA, granted priority review, or set an action date; the release asks for priority review rather than reporting a granted designation. The supporting data package is already on this site: the primary analysis from KYSA-8 was published in JAMA Neurology on November 9, 2026 (doi 10.1001/jamaneurol.2026.4155) — a pivotal, single-arm, open-label, nonrandomized Phase 2 at three US sites in which 26 of 27 enrolled patients were treated, the primary endpoint was met (timed 25-foot walk improved by a mean of −7.69 seconds at week 16, 95% CI −11.74 to −3.64, P < .001), all four secondary endpoints improved significantly, and every patient remained off immunomodulatory therapy at last follow-up. That item is not repeated here; what is new is the regulatory filing built on it. Additional one-year biomarker data from KYSA-8 will be presented at ASH 2026 — a scheduled presentation, so no biomarker figure is stated here. The rest of the franchise: KYSA-6, the Phase 2/3 registrational trial in generalized myasthenia gravis, is recruiting with 66 participants and 22 sites, and enrollment is expected to complete in mid-2027; longer-term Phase 2 follow-up presented in September 2026 and at AANEM showed durable benefit for up to 1.5 years with no high-grade CRS and no ICANS. The company states it has elected to deprioritize the KYV-102 program to focus on miv-cel, and expects to share its development strategy in progressive multiple sclerosis by early 2027; miv-cel holds RMAT in non-active secondary progressive MS. Financials: $170.6 million in cash, cash equivalents and marketable securities at September 30, 2026, which with drawable amounts under the Oxford Finance amended loan facility is expected to fund operations into 2028; R&D expenses $27.0 million and G&A $15.9 million for the quarter; net loss $41.9 million, or $0.68 per share. Registry (ClinicalTrials.gov v2 API; robots.txt read first this session): NCT06588491 (KYSA-8) is a completed Phase 2 study of CD19 CAR T-cell therapy in SPS, 27 participants actual, sponsor Kyverna Therapeutics, actual start September 25, 2024, actual primary completion and completion June 25, 2026, last update posted July 24, 2026, three US sites. NCT06193889 (KYSA-6) is a recruiting Phase 2/3 study in gMG, 66 participants estimated, actual start August 28, 2024, estimated primary completion September 2027, last update posted July 29, 2026, 22 sites. Scope note: no SPS or autoimmune-disease product appears on FDA's approved cellular and gene therapy products list, which was fetched and checked this session.

🌐 SEC filing + company release + registryNovember 11–12, 2026
A non-viral plasmid product with a logistics advantage that no viral gene therapy can copy — no viral clearance, no capsid supply chain, and administration the urology practice already has on hand

enGene Therapeutics announced on November 11 that it will initiate a Biologics License Application (BLA) submission for detalimogene (detalimogene voraplasmid) by year-end 2026, following updated results from pivotal Cohort 1 of its ongoing Phase 2 LEGEND trial and a pre-BLA meeting with FDA at which the company says the two sides reached alignment on the efficacy-evaluable population. The trial: LEGEND Cohort 1 enrolled 125 patients with high-risk, BCG-unresponsive non-muscle invasive bladder cancer (NMIBC) with carcinoma in situ, with or without concomitant papillary disease. The efficacy figures below are based on 120 efficacy-evaluable patients as of the October 29, 2026 data cutoff. Primary endpoint: complete response at any time of 56% (95% CI 47% to 65%; n=120), improved from 54% at the April 2026 data analysis. Landmark 12-month complete response 24% (95% CI 16% to 33%; n=110), improved from 13%, with one patient pending the 12-month assessment. Preliminary Kaplan-Meier estimate of duration of response of at least 12 months at 43% (95% CI 30% to 55%), improved from 25%, and preliminary median duration of response 10.1 months, improved from 8.6 months. Median follow-up for responders increased to 12.0 months from 9.2 months. Safety: 2% of patients experienced a serious treatment-related adverse event, per the company. The framing that needs qualifying: the release says that if approved, detalimogene would be the "first and only non-viral plasmid gene therapy." That claim does not hold as written, and this bulletin does not publish it. FDA's approved cellular and gene therapy products list — fetched and read this session — already contains VOCERYQ (doruxapapogene ralaplasmid and lalikinogene sifuplasmid-gxfd), INOVIO's plasmid DNA immunotherapy for recurrent respiratory papillomatosis, which FDA granted accelerated approval for on November 1, 2026 and which this bulletin reported on October 31. A narrower version of the company's claim may well be intended — that detalimogene would be the first non-viral plasmid gene therapy in urology, or the first for bladder cancer — but the release does not make that narrower statement, and it is the company's claim to sharpen, not this bulletin's to invent. Registry (ClinicalTrials.gov v2 API; robots.txt read first this session): NCT04752722 (LEGEND) is a recruiting Phase 1/2 study of EG-70 in BCG-unresponsive high-risk NMIBC, 350 participants estimated, sponsor enGene Therapeutics, actual start April 22, 2021, estimated primary completion April 30, 2028, last update posted August 7, 2026, 102 sites across Australia, Canada, France, Germany, Italy, South Korea and others. Note the gap between the two numbers: the company reports 125 patients enrolled in Cohort 1 and 120 efficacy-evaluable, while the registry enrollment figure of 350 covers the whole study, including cohorts beyond Cohort 1, and its last update predates this announcement. Financials: the same 8-K reports preliminary FY2026 (year ended October 31, 2026) cash, cash equivalents and marketable securities of approximately $245.8 million, with final financial statements not yet prepared. Scope note: detalimogene is not on FDA's approved cellular and gene therapy products list — it is under review, not approved — and the BLA submission has not been accepted or filed with FDA.

A single intrathecal AAV dose for Rett, where the bottleneck is the manufacturing campaign and the comparability package rather than the dose

The campaign met predefined product specifications as well as comparability criteria for commercial product and clinical material, which the company says supports potential inclusion of REVEAL Part A Phase 1/2 data in the planned BLA. The company also reports a single-digit empty capsid percentage as a product-quality indicator it says contributes to patient safety. Safety across the programme: TSHA-102 continues to be generally well tolerated with no severe treatment-related serious adverse events and no dose-limiting toxicities across the REVEAL Phase 1/2, pivotal and ASPIRE trials, N=33 as of the November 2026 data cutoff. All patients in the REVEAL pivotal and ASPIRE trials have been dosed and have cleared acute post-dosing safety monitoring. What is company-stated ambition: topline data from a six-month interim analysis of the REVEAL pivotal trial, together with expected FDA feedback on the BLA submission pathway, in the first half of 2027. Additional data are to be presented at the 55th Child Neurology Society Annual Meeting. Financials: net loss $39.1 million for the quarter ended September 30, 2026, or $0.10 per share, against $32.7 million and $0.09 in the comparable 2025 quarter; $422.8 million in cash and cash equivalents at September 30, 2026, which the company expects will support planned operating expenses and capital requirements into the second half of 2028. NCT07480564 is a Phase 3 in females aged over 2 to under 4, active not recruiting, 4 participants actual, actual start May 8, 2026, estimated primary completion June 2031, last update posted August 19, 2026, 3 sites. NCT06152237 is a Phase 1/2 pediatric study, active not recruiting, 6 participants actual, last update posted October 15, 2025, 5 sites. Scope note: TSHA-102 is not on FDA's approved cellular and gene therapy products list — it is under review, not approved — and the "potential" inclusion of REVEAL Part A data in the BLA is the company's expectation, not a committed filing plan it has disclosed.

🌐 Company release + peer-reviewed publication + registryNovember 10, 2026
An allogeneic, scalable cell product with an approved-adjacent manufacturing platform and a launch decision eleven days out

Capricor Therapeutics reported on November 10 that FDA is reviewing the Biologics License Application for deramiocel, its investigational cardiosphere-derived cell therapy for Duchenne muscular dystrophy, with a PDUFA target action date of November 22, 2026. This is this bulletin's first coverage of the programme — a cell therapy in Duchenne with a decision date eleven days away. What extended the review: in August the company submitted two-year open-label extension data and additional robustness analyses from HOPE-3, its randomized, placebo-controlled Phase 3 trial, together with an integrated summary of efficacy across HOPE-2 and HOPE-3. FDA classified the submission as a major amendment, extending the review by three months to November 22, 2026. The submission also proposed a refined indication focused on upper limb impairment in patients older than nine years — the population studied in HOPE-3 and the focus of its primary endpoint. The pivotal data are peer-reviewed: HOPE-3 was published in The Lancet on August 22, 2026 (408(10556):721-733, published online July 29, 2026, doi 10.1016/S0140-6736(26)01385-1, PMID 42526472) and reported a statistically significant slowing of upper limb disease progression measured by PUL 2.0 (p=0.029), with the total PUL2.0 least-squares mean percentage change at 12 months favouring deramiocel by 4.55 percentage points (95% CI 0.47 to 8.63), and supportive results across additional functional and cardiac measures. The trial was randomized, double-blind and placebo-controlled, with 106 patients in the intention-to-treat population (54 deramiocel, 52 placebo). The two-year extension data are company-reported and the comparison is not randomized: presented as a late-breaking poster at the 31st World Muscle Society Congress in October, patients who switched from placebo to deramiocel after year one declined 1.56 points less on the PUL 2.0 scale than in their year on placebo — a 76% slowing, which the release itself describes as an open-label, cross-phase comparison. Cardiac data: on November 7 Jonathan Soslow of Vanderbilt University Medical Center presented results showing slower progression of myocardial fibrosis with deramiocel versus placebo and, in patients with cardiomyopathy, left ventricular ejection fraction favoring deramiocel, which the release says were generally consistent when compared with a matched natural-history group. The HOPE-3 cardiac results received the American Heart Association Outstanding Research Award in Pediatric Cardiology. An inspection the item should not omit: as part of the BLA review FDA conducted a Bioresearch Monitoring inspection in July 2026 and issued a Form 483 with one observation; the company says it has submitted its response and is awaiting FDA feedback. Manufacturing: the GMP facility in San Diego is operational and positioned to support an initial commercial launch if the product is approved, with a second-floor cleanroom expansion underway and validation targeted for the first half of 2027. The company reports cash, cash equivalents, restricted cash and marketable securities of approximately $199 million as of September 30, 2026. Regulatory engagement in Europe and Japan has begun. Registry (ClinicalTrials.gov v2 API; robots.txt read first this session): NCT05126758 (HOPE-3) is an active, not recruiting Phase 3 randomized double-blind placebo-controlled trial of allogeneic cardiosphere-derived cells in Duchenne muscular dystrophy, 106 participants, sponsor Capricor Inc., actual start June 22, 2022, actual primary completion June 18, 2025 and estimated completion March 2027, last update posted August 28, 2026, 20 US sites. Also active are NCT04428476 (HOPE-2 open-label extension, Phase 2, 13 participants, last update posted September 22, 2026, five US sites) and NCT03406780 (HOPE-2, completed Phase 2, 20 participants, last update posted May 28, 2025, nine US sites). Scope note: deramiocel is not on FDA's approved cellular and gene therapy products list — it is under review, not approved — and every timeline above is the company's projection rather than a validated schedule. On the PDUFA date: this bulletin makes no "first" or "only" claim about it. A registry sweep of Duchenne-condition interventional studies returns 24 records; after removing five that are Capricor's own and two that matched on text only — SAT-3247, an oral AAK1 inhibitor, and PGN-EDO51, an exon-51 oligonucleotide — the remaining Duchenne cell-therapy programmes are UC-MSC, EN001 (ENCell) myoblast-derived cells, MyoPAXon (iPSC-derived muscle progenitor cells) and a set of older mesenchymal-stromal-cell and bone-marrow transplant studies. None of them is a cell-therapy BLA in the registry, and none of the registry records carries a regulatory action date at all, so this sweep cannot settle a "first to reach a PDUFA date" question in either direction.

🌐 Company release + registryNovember 11, 2026
An off-the-shelf dual-target product that needs no autologous slot — the manufacturing read is inventory, not release testing

ImvivaBio presented new follow-up data for CTA313, its investigational dual-targeted CD19 and B-cell maturation antigen (BCMA) allogeneic CAR T-cell therapy, in four poster presentations at the American College of Rheumatology Convergence 2026, held November 6 to 11 in Orlando. The pooled Phase 1 lupus dataset: 14 heavily pretreated patients with refractory SLE and minimal renal involvement, who had received a median of five prior therapies and 86.7% of whom were on concomitant tacrolimus. CTA313 showed dose-dependent expansion across the 3, 6 and 10 × 10⁶ cells per kg dose levels. B cells fell from a median of 52.2 to 0 cells per µL by Day 7 and reconstituted around Day 42 with a predominantly naive phenotype; anti-double-stranded DNA antibodies became undetectable by Month 3; and complement component C3 rose from 0.65 to 1.34 g/L by Day 28. Efficacy, with denominators as the release states them: SLE Responder Index-4 response reached 91% and Definition of Remission in SLE (DORIS) remission reached 73% at Month 6 (n=11), while DORIS remission reached 88% at Month 12 (7 of 8 patients). At Month 6, 92% of evaluable patients (n=12) were taking glucocorticoids at 5 mg per day or less, and 92.9% (13 of 14) were off immunosuppressants at the data cutoff. The immune-reset analysis: reconstituted B cells were more than 95% naive, single-cell analysis of two patients showed no overlap between pretreatment autoreactive clones and the newly formed clones, anti-double-stranded DNA antibodies remained undetectable for up to one year, and complement levels normalized. In that dataset 69% of patients reached low-dose glucocorticoid use, immunosuppression-free remission occurred in 61% of patients with SLE or LN and in 90% of those with non-renal SLE, DORIS remission exceeded 70% from Month 3 through Month 12 in non-renal SLE, and 50% (7 of 14) of LN patients achieved complete renal remission at Month 4. Safety: CRS occurred in 35.7% of patients, all Grade 1, with no neurotoxicity and no graft-versus-host disease; the most common Grade 3 or higher adverse events were neutropenia (71.4%), thrombocytopenia (21.4%) and infections (21.4%). A third poster compared single-target CD19 (n=17) and dual-target CD19/BCMA (n=27) allogeneic CAR T-cell approaches from separate Phase 1 studies and reported deeper B-cell eradication with the dual-target product; because the two cohorts come from separate studies rather than a randomized comparison, that is a cross-study comparison and is reported as such. Registry (ClinicalTrials.gov v2 API; robots.txt read first this session): NCT07783269 is the ALLNEW Phase 1b multi-center basket study of CTA313 in active autoimmune diseases — not yet recruiting, 81 estimated participants, sponsor Imviva Bio, estimated start December 2026, estimated primary completion June 2029 and completion September 2029, last update posted August 24, 2026, and zero locations listed. The 14-patient pooled dataset above has no separate registry record with results posted. Two cautions carried in the item: the 88% Month 12 DORIS figure rests on eight patients, and the 14-patient cohort is a heavily pretreated, low-renal-involvement population, so these are not generalisable SLE or LN results. First coverage on these pages: this is the bulletin's first published item on CTA313. The programme was screened and held on November 2, when the only available data were from an October 21 NeurologyLive report and the registry showed only the not-yet-recruiting basket study — so nothing was published then. This is also a different readout from the progressive-multiple-sclerosis dataset the programme generated in October, and a different indication and a different dataset. Which document each number comes from: the figures above are the company release's, and the release reflects a later data cut than the published ACR abstract for the same pooled analysis (abstract 0260, Poster Session A, sessions 0253-0287, Sunday November 8). The abstract, with a data cut of 1 June 2026, reports CRS in 28.6% of patients (all Grade 1), DORIS remission of 80% at Month 12 (4 of 5), median follow-up 11.3 months, and Grade 3 or higher neutropenia in 64.3%, lymphopenia in 42.9% and infections in 2 patients, with 85% of patients reaching low-dose glucocorticoid use. The company release reports the later cut on the same 14 patients — CRS 35.7%, DORIS 88% at Month 12 (7 of 8), neutropenia 71.4% — so a reader comparing this item against the abstract will find different numbers for the same cohort, and the release figures are the ones published here.

The same decellularised-vessel platform that carries an approved product now aimed at a much smaller and harder coronary diameter

Humacyte announced on November 9 that it has initiated the Phase 2a study of its investigational coronary tissue engineered vessel (CTEV) in patients undergoing coronary artery bypass grafting, and that the CTEV and the company's regenerative medicine platform were highlighted in three presentations at the American Heart Association Scientific Sessions 2026 in Chicago on November 7 and 8. Study design: the study evaluates the safety and efficacy of the CTEV as a vascular conduit in ten adult patients with coronary artery disease. Patency is assessed two months after implant and patients are followed for up to three years. FDA accepted Humacyte's IND in July 2026. Why this is in scope for this bulletin: the CTEV is produced using the same manufacturing system as Humacyte's FDA-approved acellular tissue engineered vessel, and SYMVESS (acellular tissue engineered vessel-tyod) appears on FDA's approved cellular and gene therapy products list. The approved product is the vascular-access ATEV; the coronary indication is a separate, unapproved product, and the trial is early. A discrepancy that is preserved rather than resolved: the company says the study "is now open for enrollment" and its third-quarter release says the company "look[s] forward to enrollment of the first patients." The registry record does not agree yet: NCT07730528 still reads NOT_YET_RECRUITING — Phase 2a, 10 estimated participants, sponsor Humacyte, estimated start September 2026, estimated primary completion and completion September 2030, last update posted July 28, 2026 — and it lists zero locations. Both statements are reported here as they stand; the registry record is a month stale and has no sites, so the company's enrollment claim cannot be confirmed from the registry. The product rationale, in the company's framing: more than 400,000 coronary arterial grafts are placed each year in the United States; the current conduits are autologous vessels, most often the saphenous vein, whose one-year graft patency is often as low as 75%; harvesting the vein carries wound-complication risk such as infection; and some patients have no adequate vein available. The CTEV is a small-diameter bioengineered vessel designed as an off-the-shelf conduit, designed to be repopulated by the patient's own cells after implantation. Preclinical results from porcine and ovine CABG models were presented on November 8. Commercial context from the November 10 third-quarter release: Symvess sales reached $1.0 million in the third quarter, a 151% increase over the second quarter, with the number of hospitals ordering Symvess up 44% from 34 to 49 as of September 30, 2026 and October 2026 sales of $0.8 million; a supplemental BLA for the ATEV in dialysis access is on track for filing with FDA in November 2026, supported by results from three Phase 3 studies, per the company.

Editas Medicine reported on November 9 that it has received Human Research Ethics Committee approval and completed the Clinical Trial Notification process with Australia's Therapeutic Goods Administration to initiate the Phase 1/2 Strive study of EDIT-401 in patients with heterozygous familial hypercholesterolemia in Australia. EDIT-401 is an in vivo gene editing medicine — the edit is delivered to the liver, so there is no autologous manufacturing step and no cell product in the supply chain. Preclinical data presented at the AHA Scientific Sessions in November, as the company reports them: LDL-C reduction of at least 90% with EDIT-401 was maintained over 10 months in an ongoing study in non-human primates; about 90% mean reductions in Lp(a) and ApoB were maintained over three months in an ongoing non-human-primate study; and the company reports a consistent safety profile with targeted delivery to the liver, transient elevations in liver function tests, no histopathologic findings and no evidence of genotoxicity in relevant primary human cells. Every efficacy number here is from non-human primates or in vitro material. The company's own superlative, attributed: IASO describes IASO208 as "the first lentiviral-vector-based in vivo CAR-T from China to receive an FDA IND clearance." That framing is the company's and is reproduced here as a company claim, not adopted as an independent finding. A bounded registry sweep, with the predicate stated, so the claim can be judged rather than repeated: the sweep required an interventional study in which "lentivir" appears in the official title, intervention name or intervention description, "CAR" or "chimeric" appears in the same text, and the phrase "in vivo" appears in the title or the intervention name. Six records qualify, all with sites in China and none with US sites. The sweep therefore shows that other China-based in vivo lentiviral CAR-T programs are registered, and it cannot adjudicate FDA-clearance chronology at all — so it neither confirms nor contradicts the "first from China" framing. Vittoria Biotherapeutics announced on November 10 that FDA has granted both Regenerative Medicine Advanced Therapy (RMAT) designation and Orphan Drug Designation to Senza5 CART5 (VIPER-101), its investigational CD5-edited autologous CAR T-cell therapy, for relapsed or refractory CD5-positive peripheral T-cell lymphoma. The RMAT designation was based on clinical data from the Phase 1 VIPER-101 study. What the product is: a dual-population CAR-T therapy produced with the company's Senza5 platform — CD5-edited, CD5-directed CAR-T cells designed for antitumor activity, plus companion CD5-negative, CAR-negative T cells designed to support immune reconstitution. The therapy is manufactured using a five-day process, and the platform is licensed exclusively from the University of Pennsylvania. What the designations mean, and what they do not: RMAT provides opportunities for enhanced interaction with FDA to facilitate efficient development and review; Orphan Drug Designation carries development incentives including tax credits for qualifying clinical trials, exemption from certain FDA user fees, and the potential for seven years of US market exclusivity if the therapy is subsequently approved for the designated use — the release states that this designation was granted in July 2026. A designation is not an approval, and a priority-review or accelerated-approval pathway is not granted by either of these designations. The release says the therapy may be eligible for priority review and accelerated approval if applicable criteria are met, and the chief executive says the company looks forward to working with FDA under the RMAT designation to advance Senza5 CART5 into a pivotal study — both are company statements about a future, conditional path. Data timing: results from the Phase 1 VIPER-101 trial will be presented in an oral session at the 68th ASH Annual Meeting on December 14, 2026. The abstract data are not yet public, so no efficacy or safety figure is stated in this item — the five-day manufacturing claim, the dual-population design and the ASH date are company-sourced and the clinical results are not yet readable. Registry (ClinicalTrials.gov v2 API; robots.txt read first this session): NCT06420089 is a recruiting Phase 1 first-in-human study of Senza5 CART5 in T-cell non-Hodgkin lymphoma, 30 estimated participants, sponsor Vittoria Biotherapeutics, actual start October 4, 2024, estimated primary completion August 30, 2028 and completion August 30, 2029, last update posted November 10, 2025 with status last verified in July 2025, and two US sites — Columbia University Irving Medical Center in New York and the University of Pennsylvania Abramson Cancer Center in Philadelphia. Note the staleness: the record's last update predates the designations by a year, so the registry does not yet reflect the pivotal-study intent described in the release.

An AAV-plus-LNP combination where the vector carries the transgene and the nanoparticle carries the editor — a delivery-partnering problem, not a cell-therapy one

Metagenomi Therapeutics announced on November 10 that FDA has cleared the Investigational New Drug application for MGX-001, its lead wholly owned program for hemophilia A. The mechanism is the interesting part: MGX-001 is designed to site-specifically integrate a Factor VIII (FVIII) gene into a "safe harbor" location within an intron of the albumin gene, using the body's own albumin promoter to drive endogenous FVIII production — a departure from approved hemophilia A gene therapies that rely on episomal FVIII expression. The product is an AAV vector delivering a promoterless FVIII gene cassette as donor DNA, paired with a lipid nanoparticle carrying mRNA encoding the company's proprietary Type V CRISPR nuclease MG29-1 together with a guide RNA targeting the albumin gene. Preclinical data, as the company reports them: FVIII activity in the normal physiologic range and durable expression in non-human primates, with no detectable off-target editing. Planned trial: the INTEGR8 Phase 1/2 trial is designed as a global, multi-center, open-label, single-arm dose-escalation followed by dose-expansion study in adult patients with moderately severe to severe hemophilia A. The company states it plans to initiate INTEGR8 in the first half of 2027 and to provide initial clinical data — safety, tolerability and FVIII functional activity — in the second half of 2027, and it intends to expand the trial to hemophilia treatment centers beyond the United States subject to clinical clearances in other jurisdictions. Further preclinical data, including the most recent efficacy and safety results, are scheduled as an oral presentation (abstract 952) at the 68th ASH Annual Meeting on Monday, December 14, 2026 in New Orleans — a scheduled presentation with no results yet readable. Registry — the point that matters most here: searches of the ClinicalTrials.gov v2 API for MGX-001, for INTEGR8, and for Metagenomi as sponsor return no records at all, and the release gives no NCT number. There is therefore no registry anchor for this programme: the trial has not started, and nothing about its site count, enrolment, dates or locations can be stated from the registry because the registry does not yet hold it. That is recorded here rather than papered over. Financials from the same release: cash, cash equivalents and available-for-sale marketable securities of $101.7 million as of September 30, 2026, with runway the company anticipates will support operations into 2028; third-quarter research and development expense of $17.5 million against $25.3 million a year earlier; and general and administrative expense of $6.9 million including a $1.2 million one-time net litigation settlement. The company is also evaluating other protein-deficiency diseases using the same site-specific large-gene integration at the albumin locus, demonstrated in non-human primates in another secreted protein disorder, with no clinical-stage program disclosed.

The company release carries the poster summary. Population: as of an August 11, 2026 data cutoff, 15 patients had been infused — eight adults and three adolescents at a 50M dose, and four adults at a 100M dose. Median follow-up was 19.0 months (range 1.0–25.5) in the adult 50M cohort, 12.4 months (3.3–16.4) in the adult 100M cohort and 10.1 months (8.5–11.5) in the adolescent 50M cohort. Safety: obe-cel was well tolerated in both adult and adolescent participants, with no ICANS and no HLH or IEC-HS, and no high-grade cytokine release syndrome events. Efficacy, with the denominator stated as the release states it: clinically significant improvements in SLEDAI-2K and achievement of DORIS were seen in most participants despite severe disease activity at baseline, including 100% DORIS achievement among patients followed for a minimum of six months; among patients followed for a minimum of six months, complete renal response was achieved in 6 of 9 participants (66.7%) at any time after infusion, and one participant (11.1%) achieved partial renal response with onset at Month 7. No severe renal flares were observed. The qualification the release does not resolve: the number of patients behind the six-month DORIS denominator is not disclosed, so the 100% figure is a rate within an undisclosed subset of the 15 infused patients and must not be read as a rate over all 15. This is the specific point that last cycle's hold turned on, and it remains the point on which this item is hedged. One thing worth knowing about the two documents: the published ACR abstract for this poster carries an earlier data cutoff — 27 February 2026, with 12 patients infused (6 adults and 3 adolescents at 50M, 3 adults at 100M) and maximum CRS Grade 1 in 8 of 12 and Grade 2 in 1 of 12 — while the company release reports the later August 11, 2026 cutoff with 15 patients. The figures in this item are the company's release figures; the abstract is the source of record for the abstract number, the session and the earlier cutoff. B-cell biology: robust CAR T-cell expansion and deep B-cell depletion were observed in all cohorts after infusion, with reconstitution showing predominantly transitional and naive B-cell phenotypes; median time to B-cell recovery was 7.2 months in the adult 50M cohort, 4.3 months in the adult 100M cohort and 6.0 months in the adolescent 50M cohort. Registry (ClinicalTrials.gov v2 API; robots.txt read first this session): NCT06333483 (CARLYSLE, AUTO1-SL1) is an active, not recruiting Phase 1 single-arm study of obe-cel in severe refractory SLE, 16 participants, sponsor Autolus Limited, actual start February 2, 2024, estimated primary completion September 2027, last update posted July 31, 2026, six sites across Spain and the United Kingdom. The pivotal study is NCT07053800 (AUTO1-SL2) — a recruiting Phase 2 single-arm study of obe-cel in severe refractory SLE with active lupus nephritis, 35 estimated participants, sponsor Autolus Limited, actual start January 16, 2026, estimated primary completion August 2028 and completion October 2029, last update posted October 30, 2026, 16 sites across Brazil, Greece, the United Kingdom, the United States and Vietnam. The registry anchors above are therefore given by NCT number and study code rather than by name.
